市場疑慮拖累美股，短線聚焦 Fed 決策與財報
2026 年 9 月 15 日
一、 9/14 美股下挫、美債殖利率曲線熊市趨平，且油價大漲
1. 股票指數：
⚫ 道瓊工業指數下跌 0.29%，收 52,421.20 點；
⚫ S&P 500 指數下挫 0.48%，收 7,619.98 點；
⚫ 那斯達克綜合指數下跌 0.56%，收 26,186.41 點；
⚫ 費城半導體指數下跌 5.86%，收 11,131.28 點。
2. 當日重要個股表現：
⚫ 在科技類股方面，輝達下跌約 3.4%、美光下跌 5.3%、邁威爾下跌 7.3%；

⚫ 通訊類股方面，Netflix 上漲 3.8%、Take-Two 上漲 3.5%、Fox 上漲 4.6%；
⚫ 軟體類股方面 ，ServiceNow、Adobe、Workday 等軟體股則上漲約 4% 至
7.4%。
3. 美國債市漲跌不一、殖利率曲線走勢熊市趨平。2 年期美國公債殖利率上升 3.24 個基
點至 4.66%，10 年期殖利率上升 2.06 個基點至 4.99%，10 年期殖利率一度升破 5%，
而 30 年期殖利率則下跌約 0.76 個基點至 5.34%。短率上升，反映市場對未來美國聯
準會升息的預期。
4. 油價因中東衝突與供給風險再度升高而上漲 ，WTI 原油上漲 1.81%，收在每桶 101.86
美元，布蘭特原油則上漲 1.56%，收在 106.24 美元。
二、 下跌原因分析
1. AI 開發放緩疑慮，壓抑硬體需求預期：Anthropic 執行長 Dario Amodei 於上週末
呼籲放慢前沿 AI 能力提升速度，並提出第三方安全評估與業界協調方案，使 AI 擴

張節奏成為新的不確定因素。我們研判，半導體位於 AI 建置支出的上游，股價對新
增算力投資的成長速度尤其敏感。即使既有訂單未立即改變，只要市場降低對未來數
季增速的期待，GPU、高頻寬記憶體及網通設備供應鏈的評價便可能先行調整；目前
仍須區分投資預期降溫與實際訂單下修。
2. 中東能源設施遇襲，油價推升通膨風險：沙烏地阿拉伯能源設施及中東船舶遇襲，引
發供應疑慮，布蘭特原油期貨 9 月 14 日結算價升至每桶 106.24 美元。油價持續偏
高可能透過運輸與生產成本以及民眾實質購買力影響企業獲利；若進一步傳導至通膨

預期，將延長貨幣政策維持緊縮的時間，對股市評價形成壓力。
3. 十年美債殖利率一度突破 5%，評價與融資承壓：美國 10 年期公債殖利率盤中突破
5%，為 2023 年以來首見。殖利率上升提高未來獲利的折現率，對依賴長期成長預
期支撐評價的股票較不利。另一方面，資料中心若仰賴借款擴張，融資成本上升也會
提高投資報酬門檻，可能影響新增建置計畫，形成 AI 供應鏈面臨的另一項壓力。
4. 通膨月增回升 ， 升息前觀望氣氛加重 ：BLS 於 9 月 11 日公布 ，8 月 CPI 月增 0.4%
，高於 7 月的 0.1%，核心 CPI 月增率也由 0.2% 升至 0.3%。我們認為，短期物
價動能回升，加上能源價格偏高，提高聯準會維持緊縮政策的壓力。投資人除了評估
當次升息，也須重新衡量後續利率高點與維持時間，增加 FOMC 會議前高波動股票
的調整壓力。
5. 成長與評價雙重重估，放大半導體跌幅：本波股市回檔壓力同時來自獲利成長預期與
市場願給的評價倍數。AI 開發放緩疑慮影響未來營收想像，殖利率上行則壓低未來獲

利的現值。當股價已反映較高成長期待，只要新增訊息不如預期，即使當期營收仍強
勁，也可能出現明顯修正。後續應觀察獲利上修能否抵銷評價收縮，不能僅以跌幅大
就認定股價便宜。

三、市場看法
1. 龍頭財報仍具成長，支持中期正向看法：輝達 8 月 26 日公布的 2027 會計年度第
二季資料中心營收達 890 億美元、年增 117%，並預估第三季總營收 1,080 億美元
、上下浮動 2%。博通 9 月 2 日公布的 2026 會計年度第三季 AI 半導體營收為
167 億美元、年增 221%，並預估第四季達 217 億美元。上述實績與公司展望，是
我們對 AI 核心供應鏈維持中期正向看法的依據；惟這些財測發布於本次事件之前，
仍須追蹤後續更新。
2. 放緩方案尚待落地，需求影響須分項評估：Anthropic 執行長 Amodei 明確表示，放

緩不代表停止模型訓練，但也提出研議訓練算力等限制，因此實際影響取決於後續執
行方式。既有模型的商業使用、推論與安全測試仍可能帶來算力需求，惟能否抵銷前
沿訓練投資的放緩，必須由使用量、訂單與收入驗證。此時適合保留成長觀點，同時
提高對支出變化的敏感度。
3. 科技股仍有輪動，軟體與半導體強弱分明：9/14 當日企業軟體與資安股逆勢上漲，顯
示科技股內部仍有輪動與受惠程度的差異。我們認為，若前沿 AI 開發放緩，部分既
有軟體業者面臨的競爭及產品替代壓力可能減輕；AI 安全疑慮升高，也可能強化資安
、權限控管及企業治理需求。相較之下，半導體更直接承受新增建置支出預期變化。
因此，9/14 當日費半重挫尚不足以推論整體科技需求同步轉弱；後續宜比較訂單能見
度、訂閱收入、獲利與現金流，單日相對強勢仍須由基本面延續性驗證。
4. 升息機率約九成，會後政策態度才是關鍵：依 Reuters 於美東 9 月 14 日收盤報導
引述的 CME FedWatch 資料，本次升息一碼的機率約 90%。若美東 9 月 16 日

FOMC 確實升息 0.25 個百分點，利率區間將升至 3.75% 至 4.00%，當次升息已
大致反映於市場預期。我們認為，會後聲明及主席記者會對未來利率政策的態度才是
關鍵：是否暗示還需升息、利率須維持高檔多久，以及是否擔心能源漲價向核心通膨

擴散，並須搭配點陣圖判讀。若後續緊縮訊號未超出預期、長債殖利率趨穩，有利評
價修復；若利率路徑較預期更高、更持久，科技股仍可能承壓。
5. 通膨改善仍不均衡，避免過早判定轉折：8 月核心 CPI 年增率為 2.4%，低於 7 月
的 2.5%，但其月增率由 0.2% 升至 0.3%，顯示年增放緩與短期動能回升並存。後
續須觀察能源漲價是否向服務價格與通膨預期擴散。若油價回穩、核心物價再度降溫
，利率壓力可望緩和；若能源衝擊持續，股市調整時間可能拉長。
四、投資建議
1. 短線聚焦 Fed 決策與 10 月美股財報，科技股中期向上趨勢料不致有變：我們目前仍

維持 AI 核心供應鏈中期正向看法，較看好能將營收成長轉化為獲利與現金流的企業
，但宜密切觀察本週的 FOMC 會議結果與 10 月中將起跑的美股財報。若雲端業者維
持資本支出、晶片公司財測穩定，且 FOMC 此次會議未釋出超預期緊縮訊號，有利
市場信心修復；然而，若出現廣泛支出下修、訂單遞延，或油價與殖利率持續上行，
則應拉長觀察期並重新檢視通膨與利率對於股市動能前景的影響。
2. 3~10 年美國公債/投資級債為債市首選：目前美國的通膨數據仍具韌性，且就業市場
穩健，使年底前市場升息預期再度升溫。市場對美國財政赤字、公債供給及長期通膨
仍要求較高風險補償，30 年期殖利率與短天期利差仍維持高檔，顯示期限溢酬尚未收
斂，考量當前 3～10 年期美國公債與投資級債能鎖定目前較高息收，且其受長端財政
風險影響也相對較小，故現階段3～10 年期美國公債及投資級債的風險報酬結構相對
較佳。

金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 10 月 13 日
上週重點訊息
◼ TOPIX 啟動大規模改革：日本交易所集團 10 月 7 日公布 TOPIX 調整結果，符合新制納入標
準的成分股將由 9 月底的 1,636 檔降至 986 檔，另有 35 檔股票新納入。現有成分股中，共
有 683 檔未符合流動性及浮動市值等標準，這些股票將自 2026 年 10 月底起逐步降低權
重，並於 2027 年 10 月重新評估，屆時仍未達標者將於 2028 年 7 月底降至零。此次改革有
助於改善 TOPIX 成分股的流動性及投資價值，並促使企業更加重視市場評價。

◼ 巴西總統選舉波索納洛首輪領先魯拉：巴西最高選舉法院 10 月 5 日公布首輪投票結果，參
議員弗拉維奧・波索納洛取得 47.03%的有效票，現任總統魯拉獲得 45.16%，兩人均未取得
過半票數，將於 10 月 25 日進行第二輪決選。若最終由波索納洛當選將相較有利於巴西股市
表現。
◼ IEA 支持加速釋出既有儲油：國際能源署（IEA）10 月 7 日表示，會員國支持加快釋出 3 月
已承諾提供的儲油，並將優先增加柴油供應。截至目前，會員國已釋出約 3.25 億桶儲油；若
剩餘承諾全數執行，可再向市場提供約 1 億桶供應，有助緩解運輸業與農業面臨的燃料成本
壓力。隨各國持續釋出戰略儲備石油，將有利於在短期內壓抑全球油價進一步上行。
市場展望
◼ 標普 500 獲利預估連續上修，資訊科技為主要推升來源：標普 500 企業獲利預估連續兩季上
修，其中資訊科技產業為主要改善來源，反映 AI、雲端運算及資料中心需求仍然強勁。雖然
美國資料中心仍面臨電力與基礎設施瓶頸，但建置規模持續擴大，AI 投資也逐步延伸至記憶

體、網路設備、資安及企業應用等領域。因此，預期科技類股的獲利動能仍具支撐，具 AI 供
應鏈優勢的台股與日股亦可望延續相關成長動能。
◼ 中天期債券可望兼顧息收與利率風險：美國經濟仍具韌性，加上政府與企業發債供給維持高
檔，可能使市場利率維持在較高水準。雖然投資級債信用利差位於相對低檔，但企業獲利、
現金流及償債能力仍具支撐，較高的美國公債基準利率也使其利息收益維持一定水準。短天
期債券面臨利率下降後的再投資風險，長天期債券則較易受到公債供給及利率波動影響，因
此中天期美國公債及投資級債可望在息收與利率風險之間維持較為平衡的表現。

展望未來，南亞科(2408.TT) 因受限於極低庫存，2027 年上半年
位元出貨量預期將保持平穩，待 4Q27 5A 新廠開出產能後，出貨
量將恢復逐步成長；產能方面，5A 新廠規劃於 2027 年第一季開
始進場裝機，第一階段預計 2028 年底前投片量達 3 萬片，全產
能 5 萬片目標於 2030 年底達成，且擴建嚴格以客戶 LTA 的 AI
專案為導向；此外，公司規劃於屏東科學園區建置3D 晶圓封裝與
測試廠，預計 2028 下半年導入設備，並與客戶合作開發客製化  
AI/WoW(晶圓堆疊)與 ASIC 專案，已開始產生部分營收貢獻，長
期目標於 2029 至 2030 年將 AI 基礎設施營收占比拉高至 40%
至 50%；技術面上，次世代 1C 製程預計於 2027 年第一季進行
客戶驗證 。 綜合以上 ， 研究部預估南亞科2027 年 EPS 為 130.72
元。考量目前公司記憶體供給吃緊、需求擴大，有長期合約支持未
來成長 ，且公司具備獨特製程升級與客製化產品線成長潛力 ， 研究
部給予 5.5 倍本益比評價，目標價 710 元，投資建議為買進。

⚫ 公司基本資料 南亞科(2408.TT)公司簡介 
南亞科技(2408.TT)成立於 1995 年，隸屬於台塑集團，為全球領先的動
態隨機存取記憶體(DRAM)製造大廠 。 公司生產基地主要位於台灣 ， 擁有
多樣化的產品組合，涵蓋 DDR5、LPDDR5/5X、DDR4、LPDDR4/4X、
DDR3 及 LPDDR3 等產品線 ， 產品種類達數十顆 ， 服務全球數百家客戶 。
面對傳統記憶體產業之劇烈循環波動，公司近年積極推動技術升級與結
構性轉型，除中長期積極投入 1B(16 奈米)與 1C(12 奈米)先進製程轉換
及新五廠建置，並深入布局企業級 SSD(eSSD)、SmartNIC、BMC 等
AI 伺服器基礎設施，同時與客戶合作開發客製化 AI/WoW(晶圓堆疊)利
基產品，正逐步由傳統大宗商品化DRAM 廠，轉型為具備高附加價值與
高敏捷度的全方位利基型記憶體解決方案供應商 。

2Q26 營運表現全面成長，售價調漲驅動獲利大幅提升 
南 亞 科 (2408.TT) 2Q26 營 業 收 入 為 825.49 億元 (Q+68.17% / 
Y+684.23%) ， 營收展現強勁成長 ， 受惠於平均銷售單價(ASP)季增超過
60%的漲勢，有效抵消了位元出貨量與上季持平的量能限制；而在 ASP 
大幅上漲且成本結構維持穩定的推升下，單季毛利率提升至
79.49%(Q+11.60ppt / Y+100.05ppt) 。雖然員工分紅與銷售研發使營業

3Q26 獲利大幅跳升，低價長約重簽驅動 ASP 季增逾六成 
南亞科(2408.TT) 3Q26 營收 1,336.49 億元(Q+61.9% / Y+611.7%)，雖
然出貨量因公司整體庫存已降至極低水位，呈中個位數季減，但整體營
收受惠於平均銷售單價(ASP)季增超過 60%(年增超過 500%)的強勁漲
勢；ASP 強勢上揚的核心主因，在於早期簽訂的低價長期合約 (LTA)於
第三季集中到期並重新議價(對 ASP 季增貢獻度超過半數)。在 ASP 大
幅 上 漲 且 生 產 成 本 結 構 穩 定 的 推 升 下 ， 單 季 毛 利 率 跳 升 至  
85.7%(Q+6.2ppt / Y+67.2ppt) 。費用方面，因增發員工薪酬獎勵，研發
費用增加至 51.45 億元、銷管費用增加至 24.43 億元，合計營業費用
為 75.88 億元 。 綜合以上 ， 南亞科(2408.TT) 3Q26 營業收入為 1,336.49
億元， Q+61.90%/Y+611.70% 、 毛 利 率  85.70% ，
Q+6.21ppt/Y+67.25ppt、 營業利益率 79.98%，Q+6.30ppt/Y+74.02ppt、
稅後純益 876.27 億元，Q+74.58%/Y+虧轉盈、EPS 為 25.40 元。

2026 年受惠 DRAM 報價持續上漲與 AI 伺服器需求，獲利創歷史新高 
展望 2026 年全年，南亞科(2408.TT) 前三季累計淨利已高達 1,639 億
元，累計EPS 達 49.28 元，公司維持 2026 全年位元銷售量成長高十
位數百分比的目標；合約與定價機制上，公司目前長約(LTA)覆蓋率落在 
50% 至 70% 之間，其中 3 年期以上長約占總出貨量 20% 以上，履
約率高達 100%，預期第四季 DRAM 供需緊缺狀態延續，合約平均售
價保持溫和成長；需求端方面，GPU、TPU、CPU 及 ASIC 等 AI 基
礎建設建置強勁，推升 AI 伺服器與基礎設施相關產品之營收占比突破 
20%； 產品組合中 DDR4 與 LPDDR4 合計營收占比超過 60%，DDR5 
占比約 10%。管理層指引目前產業短期內難以緩解供給壓力，隨著第四
季 DRAM ASP 持續看漲 ， 公司毛利率表現有機會再進一步拉升 。 綜合以
上，研究部預估南亞科(2408.TT) 2026 年營業收入為 4,190.95 億元，
Y+529.40%、毛利率  82.61%，Y+60.10ppt、營業利益率  76.83%，
Y+68.96ppt、稅後純益 2,642.16 億元，Y+3,894.93%、EPS 為 76.58
元。

長鑫存儲(CXMT)技術進展與產業影響 
長鑫存儲(CXMT)於 2026 年 9 月宣佈第五代 DRAM 制程(G5)進入量產階
段。儘管目前 G5 產品仍處於客戶驗證與良率提升階段，距離大規模商
業化出貨尚需時間 ， 但已顯示中國DRAM 技術發展速度明顯超出市場原
先預期。 長鑫 HBM3E 良率已達 40%左右。市場普遍認為，長鑫在無法
取得 EUV 設備的情況下，主要透過 ArF DUV 浸潤式曝光搭配多重曝光
(Multiple Patterning) 技術推進制程微縮，因此雖然能夠達到接近國際大
廠先進 DRAM 節點的設計能力 ， 但製造成本 、 良率與生產效率仍與三星 、
SK 海力士及美光存在差距。未來能否持續提升至國際大廠 70%以上的
量產水準，將是觀察長鑫HBM 競爭力的重要指標。短期而言，長鑫技術
進步對台廠商影響有限，長鑫目前仍面臨良率提升與客戶驗證所需時
間，短期內難以快速增加有效供給，因此全球 DRAM 供應緊張的局面仍
將延續至 2027 至 2028 年。相較之下，三星、SK 海力士與美光持續將
產能轉向 HBM 產品，反而造成 DDR4 供給逐漸退出市場，進一步支撐
DDR4 價格維持強勢，有利於以 DDR4 產品為主的南亞科營運表現；然
而，中長期則需注意，長鑫 DRAM 投片量將於 2027 年底達到約 30K 
WPM，並持續擴產，若未來 G5 良率持續改善、HBM 技術逐步成熟，
加上中國市場本土化政策支持，長鑫未來在中低階 DDR5、PC DRAM
及中國本土市場的競爭力可能快速提升 ， 進而對全球DRAM 供需格局產
生實質影響。

長鑫存儲(CXMT)4F²技術 
長鑫存儲在 10 月 12 日有表示年底推出 4F²架構 DDR5 伺服器記憶體，
4F²是將每個記憶體儲存單元配置在面積相當於 4 倍 F 平方的區域內 ， 其
中 F 代表半導體制程的最小線寬，這種設計能讓同一顆晶片容納更多儲
存單元，進而提高記憶體容量。目前，主流商用 DRAM 仍採用 6F 平方
儲存單元結構。長鑫存儲表示，其4F 平方設計的核心，在於自主研發的
垂直環繞閘極(Vertical Surrounding Gate，VSG)電晶體，這項技術透過
垂直閘極架構改善漏電問題，同時大幅縮小單元面積，提高儲存密度。
SK 海力士(SK Hynix) 已於 2025 年公佈 4F 平方技術發展藍圖，另外，
根據南韓媒體今年 4 月的報導，三星電子(Samsung Electronics)計畫於
2028 年前，量產採用這項新設計、制程低於 10 奈米的 DRAM。

結論與建議 
展望未來 ， 南亞科(2408.TT) 因受限於極低庫存 ，2027 年上半年位元出
貨量預期將保持平穩 ， 待4Q27 5A 新廠開出產能後 ， 出貨量將恢復逐步
成長；產能方面，5A 新廠規劃於 2027 年第一季開始進場裝機，第一

備，並與客戶合作開發客製化 AI/WoW(晶圓堆疊)與 ASIC 專案，已開

營業收入為 7,103.14 億元，Y+69.49%、毛利率 84.00%，Y+1.39ppt、
營業利益率 78.64%，Y+1.81ppt、 稅後純益 4,510.28 億元 ，Y+70.70%、

EPS 為 130.72 元。考量目前公司記憶體供給吃緊、需求擴大，有長期
合約支持未來成長，且公司具備獨特製程升級與客製化產品線成長潛
力，依據美光27 年本益比區間約 5 至 6.6 倍，研究部給予5.5 倍本益比
評價，目標價 710 元，投資建議為買進。

股價相對大盤走勢

南亞科 (2408 TT)  Buy

獲利處於上行，建議 Buy。  
投資建議 ： AI 運算 (GPU 、 TPU、 CPU 及 ASIC) 主導整體 DRAM
需求強勁成長，全球記憶體產業景氣循環邁入長期結構性擴張。
AI 應用領域持續擴大，推升更高容量、更高頻寬及客製化記憶
體架構需求，需求已由核心之 HBM 與 LPDDR5 全面擴展至網
路、儲存及系統管理等應用，而供應商將產能部署於 HBM 與先
進製程，對常規 DRAM 造成實質產能排擠。南亞科目前簽署長
期供應協議 (LTA) 的產能約占總出貨量的 50~70% 。不過南亞科
位元出貨量已達高點，現有廠區在 4Q27 新廠量產前產能維持
滿載，出貨量維持平穩走勢，加上公司庫存處於低檔，預估南
亞科 2027 年 bit shipment 持平，預估南亞科 2027 年稅後 EPS 
127.64 元。主要 DRAM 大廠將產能重心轉向 HBM 與 DDR5 等
高階產品，壓縮 DDR4 供給，使南亞科逐步成為 DDR4 的最大
供應商，議價能力持續提升，建議南亞科 Buy，目標價 635 元。 
3Q26 稅後 EPS 25.40 元：受惠 AI 基礎設施拉貨與長約效益浮
現，3Q26 營收為 1,336.49 億元，QoQ+61.90% ，ASP QoQ>+60% 。
匯率對營收影響 QoQ+1~3% ， bit shipment QoQ-4~-6% 。毛利率
受 Blend ASP 上升， 3Q26 毛利率上揚至 85.66% ，增加 6.17 個
百分點。南亞科 3Q26 稅後 EPS 25.40 元，淨值 118.99 元。  
預計 2026 年資本支出 697 億元 ：南亞科董事會決議為加速 5A
新廠建置並提前支付設備預付款，將 2026 年資本支出預算由原
訂的新台幣 520 億元，大幅上調至不超過新台幣 697 億元，增
加 177 億元，增幅約 34% ，較 2025 年資本支出新台幣 134 億
元，YoY 增加 420.15% ，資本支出調升主要係於 5A 新廠建置與
EUV 先進設備預付款。資本支出中 50% 用在新廠的廠務和無塵
室等，其餘 50% 則會使用在設備採購上。 5A 新廠第一階段月產
能規劃為 35.9K 片，預計於 1Q27 進行設備移入裝機， 4Q27 開
始投產貢獻出貨，目標於 2028 年底前達成每月投片量 30K 片，
並於 2030 年底前達成全產能每月投片量 50K 片之長期目標。

2 群益投顧 
  南亞科 ESG ：  
佈局綠色科技生產  
DRAM 為智慧世代的關鍵零組件，南亞科研發先進且具高效能的 「環境友善
產品」以符合市場與客戶需求。所有產品於設計初期時，即導入生命週期思
維與綠色設計，透過綠色產品推動委員會進行綠色產品管理。

重視人才發展與培養  
「人才」是公司創新、營運與永續發展的根本，強化公司全方位人才培育與
發展，南亞科技與長庚大學簽署成立記憶體學程碩士班，學生除可獲高額獎
學金，更可獲得畢業後的就業機會。南亞科技期盼從育才、用才、引才、聚
才、留才各方面，強化臺灣記憶體產業在國際上的競爭力。

共同攜手永續夥伴  
南亞科攜手上下游合作夥伴一同前行， 295 家供應商認同公司的永續理念，
一同簽署南亞科技企業社會責任承諾書且簽署率達 100% 、輔導供應商進行
永續改善，稽核、輔導與改善率均 100% 完成，並持續辦理供應鏈研討會。
此外，也與供應商共同合作推動人權及環境相關的議題。

永續績效備受肯定  
南亞科視 ESG 為企業的重要承諾，南亞科追求經營績效的同時，也同步落
實 ESG 的平衡發展。南亞科技參加 2025 第 18 屆台灣企業永續獎評選，公
司已連續第九年獲頒多項殊榮，獲獎包括年度最佳報告 (製造業 )、台灣 100
大永續典範企業獎及四項永續單項績效  – 氣候領袖獎、創新成長領袖獎、
資訊安全領袖獎與透明誠信領袖獎。  
南亞科已連續第五年入選 DJSI 世界指數，並連續第七年入選 S&P 全球永續
年鑑，持續獲得國際永續評鑑機構肯定。  
ASP 大幅增加，南亞科 3Q26 稅後 EPS 25.40 元，淨值 118.99 元：  
受惠長期合約 (LTA) 重新簽訂帶動 ASP 跳升與 AI 運算拉貨動能增溫，南亞
科 3Q26 營業收入達新台幣 1,336.49 億元， QoQ+61.90% ， YoY+611.70% 。
受長期合約重新議價影響，平均銷售單價 (ASP)QoQ>+60% ，YoY>500% ，bit 
shipment( 位元出貨量 )則因產能受限而 QoQ-4~-6% ，匯率變動則產生 1~3%
之有利影響。  
長約到期的重新簽約為近期單價上漲的主要動力 。 3Q26 平均售價 (ASP) 季增
超過 60% ，其中超過一半的漲幅來自於半年前至一年前簽訂的高涵蓋率長
約到期並重新定價。  
終端應用結構方面， AI 基礎設施 (含伺服器 )營收貢獻顯著攀升，佔比已正式
突破 20%，其餘出貨則涵蓋儲存 SSD、網通、車用工控與消費性電子等多元

將 2026 年資本支出預算由原訂的新台幣 520 億元，大幅上調至不超過新台
幣 697 億元，增加 177 億元，增幅約 34% ，較 2025 年資本支出新台幣 134

舊費用將較 2025 年減少 10% 左右，等到新廠 2027 年量產後，折舊費用才
會再增加。在位元出貨方面，現有廠區在 4Q27 新廠量產前產能維持滿載，

shipment 約 YoY+17~19% 。

2029 年 4 年不超過 3,466 億元的資本支出上限，全力建設 5A 新廠， 5A 新
廠全期規劃月產能達 50K 片，將導入 10nm(1B-1E) 先進製程及 EUV 技術。

4 群益投顧 
5A 新廠第一階段月產能規劃為 35.9K 片，預計於 1Q27 進行設備移入裝機，
4Q27 開始投產貢獻出貨，目標於 2028 年底前達成每月投片量 30K 片，並
於 2030 年底前達成全產能每月投片量 50K 片之長期目標。  
此外，公司已規劃於屏東科學園區建置 3D 晶圓封裝與測試廠，全面支援先
進記憶體架構封裝需求。屏東廠長期總資本支出估計高達 100 億美元，預計
於 2H28 開始進行設備移入與裝機。南亞科 (前段晶圓級 )主導前段的晶圓級
處理 (Wafer-level processing) 與晶圓級測試 (Wafer-level testing) 。由合作夥伴
福懋科負責後續的晶圓研磨、切割、打線、晶片封裝與最終測試。  
受惠技術自主研發與客製化合作深化，南亞科藉由靈活之產能配置積極應
對市場規格演進。產品布局上維持高度多樣化策略，同時供貨 DDR5 、
LPDDR5/5X 、DDR4、LPDDR4/4X 及 DDR3 系列，針對常規應用與利基型規
格動態調配投片比例，並透過 LTA 長約機制鎖定產能與合理利潤，有效抵
禦成熟規格現貨報價波動之潛在風險。  
1B/1C/1D 和 EUV 進度  
南亞科的 10nm 製程分為 A、 B、 C、 D、 E 世代，其記憶儲存細胞 (Cell) 可進
行微縮，因此可由 1A 逐步推進至 1B 再到 1C。製程轉換照計劃進行，會視
整體景氣動態調整。 1B 製程已滿載且良率高， 2026 年產出目標占比達 40%。
1C 製程預計於 1Q27 正式交付客戶進行驗證，次世代 1D、1E 製程及極紫外
光 (EUV) 微影技術導入開發進度亦完全符合內部規劃。現有廠區在既有空間
內持續優化先進節點投片轉換，以確保 DDR5 與 LPDDR5 等高階規格產出
持續攀升；  
EUV 在 1C 和 1D 製程都有導入，甚至 1E，還是要看客戶需求，目前預期
2028 年導入採用；在興建新廠時亦會同步建置 EUV 極紫外光微影設備獨立
廠房。 1C/1D/1E 及 EUV 研發也依計畫推進。  
4Q26 成長來自價格向上，預估南亞科 4Q26 稅後 EPS 28.94 元：  
DRAM 是算力的關鍵零組件，在 AI 驅動的投資下，推動記憶體供需結構性
改變，降低景氣循環因素。 HBM、LPDDR5 與 RDIMM 需求強勁。 HBM、高
容量 DDR5 及 LPDDR5 是整體 DRAM 需求成長的主要動能，高階 e-SSD 、
BMC 、 NIC 對 DDR4 、 LPDDR4 及 DDR5 需求持續。  
一般型 DRAM 方面，高階 PC 與手機對 DDR5 及 LPDDR5 需求維持穩定；
其他消費性方面，高價消費型產品需求暢旺，低價產品需求放緩，整體而言，
AI 需求強排擠手機、 PC、車用及消費型記憶體供給，預計記憶體供給不足
預期將持續數季以上，長期 LTA ( 數年合約 )形成供需共識。  
AI 伺服器與高效能運算拉貨動能外溢，加上手機與一般運算記憶體供給端
受限， 4Q26 DRAM 產業結構性缺貨態勢維持不變。原廠庫存普遍偏低，且
供應商均依據 LTA 長約承諾進行排產，新廠建置、設備交期與新製程導入
均需要較長前置時間，整體供給彈性極為有限，供需緊俏格局為 4Q26 營運
提供基本面支撐。 4Q26 報價已確定高於 3Q26 ，南亞科多數產品合約價持續
上揚，公司對整體價格走勢樂觀，且長約占比持續提高，短單價格仍可能因
市況波動而調整。南亞科 2H26 成長主要來自 ASP，因為庫存在低點，產能
利用率已滿載，預估南亞科 4Q26 稅後 EPS 28.94 元。預估 2026 年稅後 EPS

5 群益投顧 
76.44 元。  
產能受限，預估南亞科 2027 年稅後 EPS 127.64 元：  
AI 運算 (GPU 、 TPU、 CPU 及 ASIC) 主導整體 DRAM 需求強勁成長，全球記
憶體產業景氣循環邁入長期結構性擴張。 AI 應用領域持續擴大，推升更高
容量 、 更高頻寬及客製化記憶體架構需求 ， 需求已由核心之HBM 與 LPDDR5
全面擴展至網路、儲存及系統管理等應用，而供應商將產能部署於 HBM 與
先進製程，對常規 DRAM 造成實質產能排擠，使 DRAM 供需缺口將長期持
續 ， 關鍵觀察指標為北美CSP 及大型語言模型開發商的 AI 投資回報率 (ROI)
是否可持續。  
傳統應用市場在供給不足與價格調漲環境下，傳統應用產品出現規格下調
(down-specification) 或延長生命週期的狀況，低階市場部分由二手或回收
DRAM 支撐。  
DDR4 走勢：  
 供給短缺：由於主要供應商減產並將產能轉移至  HBM/DDR5 ，DDR4 市
場出現供給短缺，且此狀況預計將持續數個季度。  
 市場轉移至 DDR5 ：雖然 DDR4 市場占比逐漸變小，目前單一 DDR4 產
品仍佔整體 DRAM 市場總需求 12%~14% ， 若加計 LPDDR4 則比例更高 。
南亞科全球 DRAM 市佔率約為 3%，因此相對龐大的 DDR4 需求，為公
司提供相當充沛且健康的銷售空間。  
 需求穩固：消費性電子是 DDR4 需求最穩固的領域。 8Gb 容量是目前市
場的最佳選擇點 (sweet spot) 。每個客戶的應用中，可能有完整的基礎設
施 (平台 )，包括 PCB、晶片組等等。所以，短期對客戶來說，從 DDR4 轉
移到  DDR5 相對複雜，客戶必須在基礎設施變更上花費大量的時間和金
錢，且 16Gb DDR5 價格也上漲，成本高昂，因此需求將維持較長時間。
 南亞科 16Gb DDR4 於 2H26 開始貢獻營收。南亞科具備高度彈性，可隨
客戶需求與市場效益，在 DDR4 、 LPDDR4 、 DDR5 與 AI 專案之間彈性
調配產能。   
DDR5 ：  
 AI 運算 (GPU、TPU、CPU、ASIC) 全面驅動高容量與高頻寬記憶體需求，
加上個人電腦 (PC) 領域主要採用 DDR5 ，帶動 DDR5 需求穩健成長。  
 南亞科進度：公司已開始以模組形式出貨 DDR5 ，技術進度與市場同步，
高階的 6400 Mbps 產品已準備就緒。  
 目前 DDR5 交易量約占 10% ，公司目標是維持在 10% 以上並逐步提升，
但仍需視整體缺貨結構而定，短期內不會過度拉升產出，以避免對客戶
造成調整壓力。 DDR5 8Gb R-DIMM 主要應用於雲端與伺服器，但在既
有產能未擴增的情況下，大量出貨仍需等待新廠投產後才有空間。

Wafer-on-Wafer (WoW) 堆疊技術：

### Page 6

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 10 月 13 日

6 群益投顧 
 公司正積極開發  WoW 技術，以應對未來  AI/AGI 應用所需的高頻寬與
高密度需求。  
 目前相關設備已安裝，並正與多家客戶合作開發不同領域的終端產品，
但因保密協議無法透露具體應用。預估 2027 年有營收貢獻。  
 南亞科正與多位客戶合作開發客製化  DRAM ，其中也包含  HBM 的延
伸產品以及  Wafer-to-Wafer 封裝專案。由於具體進度取決於客戶端的
市場開發時程，目前尚無法給出明確的營收貢獻指引  
 台灣擁有領先晶圓代工廠 (如台積電 )，這的確有助於簡化並推動南亞科
在客製化專案 (如  HBM 及  Wafer-to-Wafer) 上的進行。  
南亞科的長期合約 (LTA) 佔總出貨量的涵蓋率約落在 50%~70% 。 合約期限從
單季到 3 年不等，其中 3 年期的超長約已佔出貨量  20% 以上。隨著 AI 與伺
服器客戶積極爭取長期產能保障，南亞科正持續與客戶推進更長天期的供
應合約，計畫逐步將過去短天期的 1 年期合約，往 3 年以上的「長期供應合
約」進行結構性轉換，以鎖定長期合作夥伴的份額。為應對記憶體產業的週
期性波動，南亞科正與客戶洽談導入包含 「價格底線與上限」 的合約機制 (如
保證採購、價格底線或預付款 )，在保障供應商合理利潤的同時，也為客戶
鎖定成本上限。這些長期合約不僅涵蓋現有的廠房產能，也涵蓋未來新廠的
整體產能規劃。一般來說，如果是季度合約，價格會在一個季度內維持固定，
雙方都會遵守約定，並在下一次談判週期到來前盡量不作調整。  
產業結構性轉變擴大需求缺口，加速多元創新  AI 解決方案，記憶體需求
維持熱絡， AI 相關需求降低景氣循環波動， AI GPU /CPU/ASIC 晶片開發持
續推升 HBM 、 LPDDR5 及 DDR5 需求大幅成長。 AI 基礎建設帶動  eSSD 、
SmartNIC 與 BMC 採用更高容量、更高效 DRAM ，以因應高速資料處理、網
路傳輸及系統管理需求。非 AI 應用產能受限，有利於高階市場發展，使終
端產品售價提升。  
南亞科預期供需緊缺的狀況預計將持續延續至 2027 年甚至 2028 年初。南
亞科目前的策略則是技術自主、長約鎖定與利潤導向的防禦性資本支出，而
非衝產量、殺價拼市占。南亞科目前簽署長期供應協議 (LTA) 的產能約占總
出貨量的 50~70% 。不過南亞科位元出貨量已達高點，現有廠區在 4Q27 新
廠量產前產能維持滿載，出貨量維持平穩走勢，加上公司庫存處於低檔，預
估南亞科 2027 年 bit shipment 持平，價格走勢維持高檔，使南亞科毛利率
水準有望優於 2026 年，預估南亞科 2027 年稅後 EPS 127.64 元。

10 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

Update
Chroma Ate Inc.  | Asia Pacific
September  Sales  +22% MoM/
+206% YoY
In this report, we focus on Chroma's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for share price movement.
Details:
• September revenue was NT$6,860mn (22% MoM/+206% YoY), a record
high.
• 3Q26 revenue came in at  NT$17,926mn (+32% QoQ/+180% YoY),
significantly higher than  our estimate at NT$14,772mn (+9% QoQ/+130%
YoY) and consensus at NT$14,555mn (+8% QoQ/+127% YoY).

Our view:
• We believe that the strength in 3Q26 was mainly driven by the
semiconductor and photonics business, especially SL T, metrology,
transceiver/CPO testing, etc.
• We think that Chroma might start to ship FT handlers and ovens in small
volume from late 2026/early 2027, which represent new business
opportunities for the coming years.
• Stay OW.

### Page 3

M
Update
Morgan Stanley Research 3
Valuation Methodology and Risks
Chroma Ate Inc. (2360.TW)
Base case, 45x 2027e P/E. We use P/E as our valuation methodology because we think it better
captures Chroma's earnings growth momentum. The stock traded at an average valuation of
~19x P/E during 2014-25 with an operating profit CAGR of 20%, implying a PEG of 1.0. We think
our target multiple of 45x is justified by the 61% operating profit CAGR we project for

2025-28.
Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected macro conditions
n Stronger semi and photonics and power testing demand
n Margin improvement via better product mix
n Better opex control
Risks to Downside
n Macro slowdown
n Softer-than-expected AI capex
n Slower progress in the advanced packaging capacity expansion
n Weaker consumer electronics and automobile related demand
n Margin contraction arising from lack of revenue scale and increasing opex

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

3-month preview: The company’s Sep revenues were up 28% YoY/10% MoM to

and rack-level AI server ramp up.

### Page 2

Earnings revision: Factoring in FOCI’s 3Q26 revenues, we revise down 2026E earnings 
by 6% on lower-than-expected CPO FAU revenues in 3Q, and revise up 2027E-30E 
earnings by 1%/1%/1%/1% mainly on higher revenues of high-speed CPO FAU, 
supported by FOCI’s focus on CPO-scale up and rising CPO switch demand.

Valuation: We continue to derive our 12m target price via a discounted P/E and derive 
the target P/E multiple via peers’ trading P/E vs. forward year EPS YoY (methodology 
unchanged). With this, our target multiple declines slightly to 16.4x (vs. 15.4x previously) 
based on FOCI’s largely unchanged long-term earnings growth (2030E-31E EPS YoY at 
15% on avg.), reflecting improving sentiment on rack-level AI server ramp up and 
high-speed transition. We continue to apply the target P/E to 2030E EPS (unchanged), 
and discount it back to 2027E via a 12.6% COE (unchanged). Our new TP is NT$900 (vs. 
NT$833 previously). Our new TP implies 51x 2027E P/E. Our new TP implies 10x 2027E 
P/S, within its historical trading range of 5x to 30x. Maintain Buy.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.
Not Rated (NR). The investment rating, target price and earnings estimates (where relevant) are removed pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when
Goldman Sachs is acting in an advisory capacity in a merger or in a strategic transaction involving this company, when there are legal, regulatory or
policy constraints due to Goldman Sachs’ involvement in a transaction, and in certain other circumstances. Early-Stage Biotech (ES). An investment

rating and a target price are not assigned pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when this company has neither a drug, treatment or medical device that
has passed a Phase II clinical trial nor a license to distribute a post-Phase II drug, treatment or medical device.  Rating Suspended (RS). Goldman Sachs
Research has suspended the investment rating and price target for this stock, because there is not a sufficient fundamental basis for determining an
investment rating or target price. The previous investment rating and target price, if any, are no longer in effect for this stock and should not be relied

upon.  Coverage Suspended (CS). Goldman Sachs has suspended coverage of this company.  Not Covered (NC). Goldman Sachs does not cover this
company.

### Page 3

TSMC (2330.TW)
15 October 2026 Citi Research
3
Rather than now slowing down, it’s actually getting stronger
TSMC reported an upbeat 3Q26 result with robust 4Q26 outlook. It guided for
4Q26 revenue of US$52.3–54.3 billion, representing about 14% QoQ growth and
58% year-over-year growth at the midpoint (based on FX at NT$31.8 per USD).
4Q26 GM/OPM is expected at 65–67% and 56.5–58.5%. TSMC reiterated its 2026
capex to be US$60–64 billion and said investment from 2026 to 2028 will be

significantly higher than in the previous three years, reflecting strong multi-year
customer demand, including agentic AI. It is also worth noting that TSMC plans to
begin using the High-NA EUV technology around 2030 and develop a 12-inch
mask capability by 2031, when it expects the tools to offer sufficient maturity and
cost effectiveness. Management said this timing does not undermine its
confidence in technology leadership.
Management described AI-related demand as exceptionally strong, supported by

accelerators, networking chips, and rising demand for CPUs associated with
agentic AI. TSMC’s previously guided AI GPU/accelerator CAGR to be mid-to-high
50%, but now sees more upside ahead. Given rising concern on AI expanding
peaking out, the management said it is clear that its capacity planning strategy has
considered customer forecasts, data-center construction, power availability, and
other supply-chain constraints. Management still judges demand to be above

available supply and is working to close the gap, while emphasizing disciplined
planning to limit the risk of overbuilding.
Figure 1. TSMC – 3Q26 results comparison
© 2026 Citigroup Inc. No redistribution without Citigroup’s written permission.
Source: Citi Research, Bloomberg
Capacity expansion plan and allocation
During the earnings call, TSMC also mentioned that despite the tight capacity
supply, customers cannot generally sublet or auction wafer capacity, while there is

limited case that collaboration is through mask-license agreements. For the
advanced packaging, TSMC has announced its Global Foundries and VIS’
agreement on interposer as part of its backend supply strategy, intended to help
ensure customers have enough capacity to bring products to market; It also
reiterated that TSMC’s CoWoS remains as the leading customer’s choice and is
already in volume production at 5x reticle size, while it is developing 9x and larger

solutions and evaluating CoPoS.

### Page 4

TSMC (2330.TW)
15 October 2026 Citi Research
4
Reiterate Buy, best position in AI arena
Our earnings projection is largely unchanged for 2027 /2028 earnings. While its
capex expansion, overseas operation and N2 will likely be a margin dilution factor
initially, due to its cross-node leverage, high UTR and yield rate, we believe its GM
downside is manageable. Overall, we see strong advanced node demand into next
year and the long-term order visibility will be supported for higher capex cycle.  We

maintain our TP to NT$4,000, based on unchanged 25x of 2027 EPS estimate;
reiterate Buy.
Key TSMC earnings call recap
n4Q26 guidance
–Sales: US$52.3-54.3bn (+14% QoQ at the midpoint)
–GPM: 65-67% (assuming USDTWD=31.8)
–OPM: 56.5-58.5%
n2026 full-year & long-term guidance
–Full year sales: raise 2026 full-year USD-based sales guidance from more
than 40% growth to +44% growth
–Full year capex: retain its 2026 capex guidance at US$60-64bn (70-80%
advanced process, 10% specialty, 10-20% for advanced packaging, testing

and masks)
–GM outlook
•4Q GM to be impacted by N2 and overseas fab ramps, but partially
supported by productivity improvement, UTR enhancement and
optimizing capacity allocation
•GM dilution from overseas fab would be c2ppts impact for the full-year
2026; with more new capacity expansion in the US, the margin dilution
impact would widen to 3-4ppts in the next few years.
–Long-term outlook
•Retains 5-year AI sales CAGR (2024-2029) remains at high-50% or above

nCapacity planning
–TSMC currently has 20 fabs in total. It plans to add 13 fabs in Taiwan and add
5-6 overseas fabs.
–N2 capacity will double in 2027; it has also started preparing for A14 capacity
with volume production starting from 2028.
–US Arizona fab 2 construction is complete and begin volume production in
3Q27, ahead of schedule.
–JP fab 2 would produce N3 with mass production from 2028

### Page 10

TSMC (2330.TW)
15 October 2026 Citi Research
10
TSMC
Company description
TSMC is the founder and leader of the dedicated IC foundry segment. The
company has built its reputation by offering advanced and "more-than-
Moore" wafer production processes and industry-leading manufacturing
efficiency. From its inception in 1987, TSMC has consistently offered the
foundry segment's leading technologies and TSMC-compatible design
services.
Investment strategy

We rate TSMC shares as Buy. We expect TSMC to deliver sales growth of
>40% in recent years thanks to strong growth from HPC/AI as well as stable
demand growth from smartphone, Io T, and automotive on tech migration and
a shortened replacment cycle. CPU outsourcing to TSMC offers further
growth potential. A rising cash dividend should also support the share price.
While TSMC has been increasing its capex meaningfully since 2009, its cost
structure remaisn competitive through continuous product mix

improvements, efficiency enhancements, and cost reductions.
Valuation
Our target price for TSMC shares of NT$4,000 is based on 25x our 2027E
EPS, higher than its three-year average forward PER, versus global peers’
average of 16-25x. We believe our target PER is justified by TSMC's leading
position in advanced process node and robust AI demand growth outlook.
Given its continuing leadership in the semiconductor foundry industry, we
believe TSMC’s stable order visibility and earnings outlook make it less

vulnerable to a potential global economic downturn than some of its peers.
Our target price equates to 2026E/27E P/E of ~ 36x/25x and 2026E/27E P/B of
~13x/10x.
Risks
Key downside risks that could impede the shares from achieving our target
price include: 1) weakness in the global semiconductor market; 2) larger-
than-expected margin contractions due to depreciation cost increases and
strong NTD; 3) competitors entering the foundry business; 4) longer-than-

expected digestion of inventory in the supply chain; and 5) a slowdown in
demand either due to a global economic downturn or a global trade
disruption triggered by tariffs.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

### Page 1

ab 15 October 2026
Global Research
Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing
Full speed on capacity expansion, backed by a
strong US commitment
Cloud AI demand strengthening, with no signs of slowing
TSMC management remains optimistic of a stronger multi-year Cloud AI mega-trend. Its
optimism is based on its prudent and comprehensive assessment of data centre
construction, power shell build up, component supply, and the financial situation of
Cloud service operators. Its capex target was retained at US$60-64bn in 2026, implying

a meaningful uptick to US$19.4-23.4bn in Q4 vs US$40.6bn in 9M26. Our recent
research highlighted TSMC is further accelerating expansion across N3/N2/A14, and
could lift capex to US$90bn in 2027E and US$105bn in 2028E.
Stronger commitment to  US expansion
TSMC guided the overseas dilution on gross margin to rise to 3-4% in 2027 from a
narrowing to 2% (vs. prior 2-3%) in 2026 given the accelerating expansion especially in
the US. The second fab in Arizona should start mass production for N3 in Q327, slightly

earlier than prior plan. Management also stated that it's in talk with SpaceX/Terafab on
potential collaboration, with key principle being to operate the fabs by TSMC.  As what
we wrote recently, TSMC is also evaluating the possibility to expand outside  Arizona,
with Texas being an option. The greater ambition to expand global footprint and
support reshoring is key in solidifying TSMC's competitive positioning, in our view.
Better Q4 sales guidance, slightly down GM guidance not concerning

Q3 EPS of NT$29.43 was above LSEG consensus of NT$28.67, mostly due to higher sales
and OPM of 58.7%. Q3 gross margin of 66.4% was a bit below expectation of 67%
into earnings, given N2 dilution. Q4 sales is guided to grow 14% QoQ at mid point,
slightly ahead of expectation of ~10% QoQ into earnings, driving full year sales
guidance in USD to grow 44% YoY. TSMC guided greater dilution from N2 and overseas
expansion, which lowers Q4 GM 40bps QoQ to 66.0% at the mid point of guidance. We

view TSMC’s investment case on strong growth at sustainable margins with good
support in 2027  (UBS GMs up 10bps YoY at 66.7% from 66.6% in 2026) with price
hikes to pass on higher costs.
Valuation: maintain Buy and target of NT$3,650
We raise 2026-28E EPS by 2-3% to reflect higher sales even with slightly lower gross
margin assumptions. Our new 2028E EPS is 10% above consensus pre-earnings. We
view resilient risk/reward for TSMC stock at 16x 2027E PE vs 36% earnings growth in

2027 based on UBSe, and expect the stock to steadily outperform the broader tech
sector at least through early 2027, assuming the market volatility persists. Reiterate Buy
rating. Our price target is based on 20x 2027-28E PE with a 26% long-term earnings
CAGR.

Company Description
Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing (TSMC) is the world's leading dedicated foundry
service provider, with annual capacity of about 32m of 8-inch equivalent wafers as of end-
2024. It operates four 12-inch wafer fabs, four 8-inch wafer fabs and one 6-inch wafer fab in

Taiwan. TSMC also manages two 8-inch fabs and one 12-inch fab overseas under wholly
owned subsidiaries WaferTech in the US, and TSMC China and TSMC Nanjing in China.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We base our price target for TSMC on a PE multiple.
We think TSMC faces a variety of risks, including rapidly changing technology, intense
competition, high capital investment and cyclical demand. TSMC also has a history of paying
stock options to its employees, which could dilute earnings.

KEY DEFINITIONS:Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is defined as expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over
the next 12 months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA) is defined as
the one-year local market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR)
Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the

near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have an investment
horizon of 12 months.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

considerations may be lower or higher than portfolios where ESG factors, exclusions, or other sustainability issues are not considered by the portfolio manager, and the
investment opportunities available to such portfolios may differ.

Editorial 
A Fed credibility-induced bounce  
Strategy summary:  The Fed’s unanimous decision to hike 
rates this week for the first time since 2023 , and pencil in 
another hike by December, confirms our expectations – flagged 
in the last two weeklies - of a central bank willing to establish its 
independence against political pressure. The Fed’s moderately 
hawkish pivot is helping restore its inflation-fighting credentials. 
The BoJ also hiked rates this week. It is the first time on record 
all three major central banks – Fed, ECB and BoJ – have hiked 
in the same month , as they respond primarily to a resurgence 
in energy-driven inflation amid tight job markets.  
We expect the Fed to hike once more by year-end to 4.0-4.25% 
and follow up with another hike in H1 2027 to 4.25 -4.5%. With 
the revised estimates, we revise up our US government bond 
yield forecasts. Nevertheless, with Fed policy uncertainty 
easing, we see an opportunity to use the equity market dip to 
add exposure amid solid corporate earnings.  
Fed moves to restore credibility:  The Fed’s policy pivot this 
week followed Chair Warsh’s hawkish Jackson Hole speech 
and recent economic data prints, namely: a pick -up in job 
creation and inflation over the summer and a sharp rebound in 
oil prices due to the re -escalation of the Middle E ast conflict. 
Given the unanimous Fed decision to hike rates this week, we 
expect two more hikes by  June 2027 on the back of  robust 
growth amid strong AI investments and a tight labour market, 
keeping inflation elevated. We expect this pivot to help the Fed 
rebuild credibility as an inflation fighter.  
Policy outlook less aggressive than market pricing : The 
updated Fed policy rate estimates are lower compared with 
money market pricing of three more rate hikes by June 2027 
since we expect disinflation to take hold by Q2 2027 as the 
impact of oil prices and tariffs fade. We believe policy rates can 
return to the 4.0–4.25% range by end-2027, with the possibility 
of cuts in H2 2027 as inflation pressures ease.  
Upgrading near-term US rates and USD estimates : Based 
on these revised estimates, we also upgrade our 3-month target 
for the US 10 -year government bond yield to 5.0 -5.25%. We 
also revise up our 12 -month target for the US 10 -year bond 
yield to 4.75-5.0% (see page 4).  
Buying the equity dip as focus turns to fundamentals: Fed 
hikes typically do not hurt equities over the medium-term when 
accompanied by strong earnings growth. We estimate a 25bps 
rise in Fed rates (the discount rate) would impact the S&P500 
index and global equities by 3 -4%. However, we expect the 
headwind from higher rates to be offset by strong earnings 
growth, driving positive performance for global equities.  
Furthermore, a hawkish Fed is largely priced in and we expect 
the Fed to deliver fewer rate hikes than currently priced by 
markets. Meanwhile, we believe concerns over AI industry 
leaders slowing the pace of development are overdone. Hence, 
we maintain our preference for a diversified group of large cap 
technology and semiconductor industry leaders (see page 5-6). 
BoJ hikes; modestly bullish JPY : The BoJ hiked rates by 
25bps to a three-decade high of 1.25%, as expected, but the 7-
2 split vote led to renewed JPY weakness on concerns it may 
slow the pace of further hikes. We remain constructive on JPY 
amid narrowing rate differential vs peers as the BoJ potentially 
hikes rates by 25bps once per quarter until Q2 2027 to curb 
rising domestic wage-driven inflation pressure . Any sign of BoJ 
slowing the pace of hikes could lead to renewed JPY weakness, 
fuelling inflationary pressures (see page 7).  
BoE holds; rangebound GBP: The BoE held rates this week, 
bucking the hiking trend among peers, as weakening UK job 
and housing markets offset rebound in energy-driven inflation. 
We expect BoE to deliver fewer rate hikes than the 100bps of 
increase currently priced by markets  as the job market slows 
further. This is likely to keep GBP rangebound (see page 7).  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

Top client questions 
  Where do you see US government bond  yields following 
the Fed’s 16 September decision to hike interest rates? 
Our view: The US 10-year government bond yield is likely to 
retest the 5.00-5.25% range in the near term. We favour 3-7-year 
maturity bond s in t he US, preferring credit over government 
bonds. We are looking for an opportunity to extend duration 
approaching Q2 2027. 
Rationale: Over the next few months, we expect 2- and 30-year US 
government bond yields to remain rangebound, but the 10-year yield 
is expected to rebound up to the 5.00-5.25% range. The reasons for 
this include: 1) oil prices staying elevated and potentially rise further 
due to the fluid geopolitical situation in the Middle East, with Saudi 
Arabia’s East-West oil pipeline shut, the Houthis now firmly in control 
of the Strait of Bab-al-Mandab, the Strait of Hormuz still closed and 
China returning as a buyer of crude oil in the market ; 2) despite 
notable AI leaders expressing the need to slow AI development, AI 
capex plans are showing no signs of slowing down, which continues 
to drive above-trend growth in the economy. This, in turn, also means 
AI-related heavy bond issuance  is expected to continue. These 
issuers typically issue long-dated tenors, and this competition for 
capital will also exert upward pressure on the US 10-year yields. 
We note that the US 10-year bond yield has room to move lower, 
driven by short-covering activities in the very near term as net-short 
positions had moved to extreme levels before th is week’s  Fed 
meeting. However, we think such downshifts would be short-lived 
and the central tendency for the 10-year yield would be to move back 
up to the 5.00-5.25% range. 
Meanwhile, we see US 30-year yields continuing to reflect the dire 
fiscal outlook for the US government. We expect US 2-year yields 
to continue trading in a range that reflects expectations of further 
federal funds rate hikes. For the record, we only expect two more 
rate hikes from the Fed into the middle of 2027 , while money 
markets are pricing in three more for the same period. 
Going into H2 2027, we expect oil prices to moderate (either 
through a resolution of the M iddle East conflict or a re-routing of oil 
supply chains)  and the inflationary effects from tariffs to fade. 
Inflation by then is likely be close to – if not at – the Fed’s target of 
2%. At this point, we see room for the Fed to contemplate rate 
cuts. US 2-year yields (the tenor that is most sensitive to Fed policy 
changes) will likely start to price th is in. With oil prices and inflation 
cooling, 10-year yields would also have room to come down. 
US 30-year yields, on the other hand, are expected to  remain 
elevated as the US fiscal deficit remains substantial next year and 
could become much worse than its already dire state today. As a 
result, the ‘term premium ’ may keep the ultra -long end elevated. 
Short of some unorthodox policy to artificially cap them lower, 30 -
year yields could continue to drift higher into H2 2027. 
We continue to favour the 3-7-year segment of the yield curve and 
prefer credit over government bond s. We see an opportunity for 
us to extend duration closer to Q2 2027. 
—  Jonathan Liang, CFA, CIO for Fixed Income and FX

### Page 5

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 18 September 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Will the step -up in the  Fed’s risk-free rate trigger a re -
pricing of equities? 
Our view: We expect strong corporate earnings growth to offset 
the headwind of higher rates , driving positive performance for 
global equities. We remain Overweight global equities. 
Rationale: The Fed’s latest 25bps rate hike had been anticipated 
by the bond market, partly reflected in the bond yield  rise we have 
seen so far in 2026. Using the US 10-year government bond yield as 
a proxy for the risk -free rate, the yield has risen by 80-85bps this 
year. We estimate that each 25bps rise in the discount rate presents 
a valuation drag on global equities. On this basis, the rise in yields 
represents a potential 10-14% headwind for equities this year. 
However, the Fed’s hike reflects a solid expansion of US economic 
activity, resilient domestic spending and robust capital investment. 
This is consistent with the positive earnings revisions we have seen 
this year, with  2026 earnings growth projections now at 34% (and 
16% for 2027). Global equities are up 14% year-to-date (YTD), but 
even with a 14% valuation headwind from higher yields, we believe 
they can rise further, given strong earnings growth in 2026 and 2027. 
—  Fook Hien Yap, Senior Investment Strategist 
  What is the outlook for the US 10-year government bond 
yield? What is your tactical view on the DXY? 
Our view: We raise our three-month US 10 -year government 
bond yield forecast to the 5.00-5.25% range. Meanwhile, we see 
a near-term upward bias in the DXY after its recent downtrend, 
supported by recent Fed tightening and higher  short-term US 
bond yields.  
Rationale: The Fed 's decision  to raise the target range for the 
federal funds rate by 25bps to 3.75-4.00% validates the upside policy 
risks we flagged recently, following Fed Chair Warsh's Jackson Hole 
speech and a run of firmer US economic data. We expect growth 
to remain robust, the labour market  to remain tight and inflation to 
stay elevated, supporting one more hike before year-end 2026 and 
potentially another in H1 2027  – fewer than the three additional 
hikes currently priced in by money markets . For the US 10-year 
government bond yield, further policy tightening and lingering US 
fiscal concerns should keep yields elevated, but the Fed’s visible 
response to inflation should limit additional upward pressure, leaving 
the 10-year yield holding around the 5.00-5.25% range. 
Crucially, Warsh provided no forward guidance on further rate 
hikes. Markets had already priced in substantial additional rate hikes 
ahead of the Fed meeting, reducing the scope for further USD upside 
purely from rate repricing. Meanwhile, we expect further policy 
tightening from the ECB and the BoJ, while the RBA also remains 
biased towards further tightening. This should gradually narrow the 
US rate advantage and limit the extent of a DXY rebound. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Anthony Naab, CFA, Investment Strategist

Global equities’ earnings growth estimates have 
been revised higher this year, offsetting the 
headwind from higher rates

### Page 6

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 18 September 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  How should investors position in technology as AI lab 
CEOs call for a slowdown in frontier AI model development? 
Our view: We believe concerns over “pacing the frontier” are 
overdone. We maintain our preference to remain invested in 
and diversified across Big Tech and semiconductor leaders. 
Rationale: We believe it is prudent for investors to avoid 
overreacting to the recent calls for a slowdown in frontier AI model 
development, as they focus on responsible AI development, rather 
than halting AI model development or reducing spending. The global 
AI leadership  race, reinforced by the US and China’s  strategic 
ambitions, also makes a coordinated slowdown unlikely. 
While the near-term narrative may shift from AI training beneficiaries 
towards inference plays, hyperscalers continue to signal strong AI 
infrastructure commitments and improving monetisation, supporting 
our AI capex outlook. Rising AI adoption and agentic use cases 
should sustain inference demand, even if training activity moderates, 
benefiting custom AI chips, NAND flash memory and hyperscalers.  
We believe the decline in valuations  this year more than 
compensates for any downside risk to AI capex. Our Opportunistic 
idea on Global Semiconductors further reflects our conviction, with 
attractive valuations supporting selective additions during volatility. 
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist 
  With ASEAN and UK equities outperforming AI-focused 
markets in H2 2026, is it worth adding to these markets? 
Our view: ASEAN and UK equities offer portfolio diversification, 
but we are Underweight both markets in favour of US and Asia 
ex-Japan (AxJ) equities, given their stronger earnings potential. 
Rationale: The ASEAN and UK markets have outperformed the 
MSCI ACWI over the past three months, but both have 
underperformed on a YTD basis. Financials is the largest sector in 
the ASEAN (53%) and the UK (28%) and provides steady earnings. 
However, both markets have minimal exposure to the technology 
sector (just 3% and 1% , respectively), ultimately dragging down 
growth. Hence, while defensive markets offer value by increasing 
diversification and reducing volatility in a portfolio, we maintain our 
Underweight allocation for ASEAN and the UK, as we see much 
stronger earnings potential elsewhere in the US and AxJ markets.  
We remain Overweight US and AxJ, supported by robust earnings 
growth, as  AI still has the potential to become a major economic 
growth driver . Our 12-month S&P500 Index forecast of 8,400  
implies low-teens upside, supported by a strong earnings backdrop 
of 25-30% earnings-per-share growth for 2026 and 15 -20% for 
2027. AxJ also boasts the strongest earnings growth among major 
markets, fuelled by the AI semiconductor super-cycle. 
—  Cindy Lam, CFA, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 7

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 18 September 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Why is it still appropriate to have a positive stance on US 
high-yield (HY) bonds? 
Our view: We remain opportunistically bullish on US HY bonds. 
Lower sensitivity to interest rate volatility, a below-trend default 
rate and a maturity wall pushed out to 2028-29 drive this view. 
Rationale: Recent interest rate volatility has highlighted a key 
structural advantage of US HY bonds: their higher coupon rates  
and shorter tenors relative to Developed Market (DM) government 
bonds and US investment-grade (IG) corporate bonds translate into 
a much lower sensitivity to interest rates, cushioning bond prices 
during yield backup episodes, where bond yields rise sharply. 
YTD, US HY has delivered 1.8% in total returns, outperforming DM 
government bonds and US IG corporate bonds by over 3% each on 
a relative basis. This outperformance was particularly evident during 
the recent back-up in bond yields, as inflation concerns re-emerged 
after renewed Middle East tensions – an environment where HY's 
lower rate sensitivity provided a relative cushion  even as broader 
fixed income markets came under pressure in absolute terms. On 
the fundamentals side, US HY corporate credit quality remains 
resilient. Default rates continue to track below long-run trend levels, 
and the maturity wall has been pushed out to 2028 -29, reducing 
near-term refinancing risk. Against a non -recessionary macro 
backdrop, we expect these credit fundamentals to hold, supporting 
the case for continued opportunistic exposure to the asset class.  
—  Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Do the latest BoJ and BoE meetings alter your view on the 
central banks’ rate paths and their corresponding currencies? 
Our view: We remain constructive on the JPY and expect two 
BoJ rate hikes this year and potentially one hike per quarter in 
H1 2027 as policy normalisation continues. Meanwhile, the BoE 
held rates at 3.75%. We expect GBP/USD to stay rangebound 
within 1.32-1.36 over the next 1-3 months, with downside risk 
towards the lower end of our medium-term 1.30-1.36 range. 
Rationale: Persistent inflation, higher energy prices and earlier JPY 
weakness support further BoJ policy normalisation . Continued 
hiking should progressively narrow the US -Japan rate gap, reduce 
the attractiveness of JPY -funded carry trades and support  the JPY 
over the medium term towards the 153-155 range. However, with a 
September hike largely priced  in, future tightening ’s pace  will be 
more important for the JPY. Meanwhile, the BoE was split 6-3 in its 
decision to hold rates at 3.75% , with three members preferring a 
25bps hike, signalling elevated inflation risks. However, a weaker 
labour market is likely to limit the pace of BoE tightening. The US-
UK rate divergence is expected to favour the USD over the next 1-3 
months. Further BoE tightening (if inflation remains elevated ) could 
narrow the rate gap and provide support to the GBP later in the cycle. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist

We expect US HY bonds to continue delivering 
strong performance in the near term

## Document 238: AI融资成本攀升：Anthropic拟向黑石提供超10%收益率

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI融资成本攀升：Anthropic拟向黑石提供超10%收益率.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-17
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Anthropic 正推进代号 “Project North Star” 的 600 亿美元芯⽚债务融资，创历史纪录。为筹
得资⾦，公司向锚定投资⼈⿊⽯集团提供逾 10% 收益率，借贷成本仅四个⽉即显着飙升。
此举意在上市前锁定算⼒储备，以⾼息套利押注上市后通过评级提升降低成本。
摘要：
Anthropic 正以远⾼于市场基准的融资成本推进其史上最⼤规模芯⽚融资，折射出 AI 基础设施军
备竞赛背后⽇益⾼企的资本代价。
据英国《⾦融时报》 17 ⽇报道，Anthropic 正向⿊⽯集团（ Blackstone ）提供逾 10% 的收益
率，以换取后者参与这笔代号 "Project North Star" 、总规模⾼达 600 亿美元的债务融资。知情
⼈⼠透露，⿊⽯旗下基⾦将认购其中 90 亿美元，约占 180 亿美元次级贷款的⼀半，并获得 10% 的
票息，加计新发折价及其他费⽤后实际总回报更⾼。
这笔融资规模预计将创下芯⽚融资历史纪录，并将成为衡量投资者对 AI 债务胃⼝的重要⻛向标。

值得关注的是，仅仅四个⽉前， Anthropic 以 8.5% 利率完成代号 "Project Big Sky" 的约 44 亿美元
融资，借贷成本的快速攀升，凸显出投资者对 AI 企业密集融资需求的警惕情绪持续升温。
600 亿融资案：规模创纪录，成本同步飙升
"Project North Star" 旨在为 Anthropic 采购约 1.5 吉⽡由⾕歌开发的定制半导体 —— 即张量处理单
元（ TPU ） —— 提供资⾦。
整体债务结构分为两层：约 420 亿美元为优先级债务，由参与芯⽚研发的 Broadcom 提供担保；
其余 180 亿美元为次级贷款， Blackstone 认购其中 90 亿美元，剩余份额将于未来数⽉⾯向更⼴
泛投资者出售，出售利率可能低于⿊⽯所获条款。预计当资⾦于 2027 年提款完毕后，这部分债
务将进⼊机构 144A 债券市场流通。
Blackstone 拒绝置评， Anthropic 未⽴即回应置评请求。
溢价逾 300 个基点，⻛险溢价需求凸显
根据 ICE 美银 BB 级美国⾼收益债券指数，信⽤状况与 Anthropic 相近的借款⼈在公开债券市场通

常⽀付约 7% 的收益率。 Anthropic 此次向⿊⽯提供的 10% 票息，较该基准⾼出逾 300 个基点，若
计⼊折价及费⽤，实际收益差距更⼤。
这⼀溢价反映出两⽅⾯压⼒：其⼀，投资者对科技企业短期内集中释放的庞⼤融资需求愈发审
慎，要求更⾼的⻛险补偿；其⼆，⼤型融资交易通常需向锚定投资者和早期承诺⽅提供额外溢
价，以确保交易顺利落地。
借贷成本的快速上⾏，亦令 Anthropic 谋求投资级评级的路径更加复杂。评级机构 KBRA 于今年 6
⽉将 Anthropic 评定为投资级以下⼀档，本次次级债务预计将获得类似评级。
IPO 前的战略布局：⾼成本融资换⻓期降费空间
尽管融资成本⾼企， Anthropic 仍选择主动承压，背后存在明确的战略逻辑。据媒体援引知情⼈
⼠透露， Blackstone 已向其投资伙伴表⽰，此次交易是在⾼收益⽔平下投资 Anthropic 关联债务

## Document 255: Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, September (2026-10-19)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, September (2026-10-19).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#businessconditions #confidence #aud #australia
Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, September
By Luka Belobrajdic
Economist, Westpac
14:15 October 13 2026 Share
Confidence posts its sharpest fall since the start of the Middle East conflict as elevated fuel
prices and higher interest rates weigh on business sentiment, while conditions hold steady
at weak levels. Conditions unchanged at -1 / Confidence -4pts to -13.

### Page 2

The September NAB Business Survey was in the field from 23 to 30 September, capturing ongoing
global uncertainty, still-rising fuel costs and, at least in part, the RBA’s September rate hike. Business
confidence continued to deteriorate, recording its sharpest fall since March and the onset of the Middle
East conflict. The index declined 4pts to -13 and, while remaining above the lows recorded earlier this
year, is now at its weakest level since May. At 18pts below its long-run average, the result underscores

the deep pessimism that continues to weigh on businesses. This broadly mirrors the pessimism evident
in the Westpac-Melbourne Institute Consumer Sentiment Index, where elevated fuel prices and higher
interest rates have coalesced in a similarly weak assessment of the outlook.
The headline business conditions index held steady in negative territory at -1, albeit rising 0.25pts on
an unrounded basis. While encouraging that conditions have not deteriorated further, the index has

now remained at -1 (rounded) for two consecutive months, its weakest level since the height of the
COVID-19 pandemic in 2020. At 7pts below its long-run average, the result points to continued
pressure across the business sector.
Across the major sub-components, employment moved into negative territory, falling 3pts to -1 and
sitting 4pts below its long-run average. This marks the first outright negative reading since November
2020 and provides further evidence of the gradual easing in labour market conditions. Against the

backdrop of compressed margins, the result may also indicate firms are becoming increasingly
cautious around hiring decisions. By contrast, trading conditions rose 1pt to 4, although they remain
8pts below their long-run average. The relative resilience in trading conditions is broadly consistent with

### Page 3

Westpac DataX Card-Tracker data, which suggests nominal spending remained resilient, albeit
slowing, through the September quarter despite ongoing headwinds facing consumers. Profitability
recorded the strongest improvement across the major components, rising 3pts to -5, although the sub-
index remains deeply subdued at 10pts below its long-run average.
Capacity utilisation edged 0.2ppt lower to 82.3%. While the monthly decline provides a tentative signal

of easing capacity pressures, utilisation remains 0.9ppt above its long-run average and continues to
trend higher on a three-month average basis. Capital expenditure continued to recover from its mid-
year weakness, rising 3pts to 7. While still below the highs recorded in the first quarter, the sub-index is
now trending higher on a three-month average basis, pointing to some stabilisation in investment
intentions.
Cost growth was comparatively subdued in September, although purchase cost pressures remain

elevated. Purchase cost growth edged 0.1ppt higher to 2.4%qtr, sitting 1.1ppt above its long-run
average and continuing to weigh on margins. By contrast, labour cost growth eased 0.1ppt to 1.7%qtr,
although it remains 0.5ppt above its long-run average. Final product price growth was unchanged at
0.8%qtr, 0.2ppt above its long-run average. The widening gap between purchase cost and final product
price growth suggests firms continue to absorb a meaningful share of higher input costs, consistent

with profitability remaining deeply below average. Looking ahead, the October survey will provide an
early indication of how the card surcharging ban is affecting business costs and the extent to which
these are passed through to final prices.

### Page 4

Related articles
Among other indicators, forward orders declined 1pt to -1, pointing to continued caution around the
demand outlook. Inventories, by contrast, rose 4pts (rounded) to 7, some 5pts above their long-run
average. In combination with weak forward orders, the inventory build points to a degree of slack
emerging across the business sector.

## Document 328: Indonesia_ Policy mix aligns (2026-10-12)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Indonesia_ Policy mix aligns (2026-10-12).pdf

Date: 2026-10-12

### Page 1

SHIFT IN NARRATIVE

After a rough first half of the year, the 
narrative around Indonesia’s economy has 
turned relatively constructive . The macro 
outlook is on the mend, and policy coherence 
has strengthened. Recent budget 
developments have reduced the likelihood of an 
outright fiscal slippage. A more disciplined 
fiscal trajectory should also provide room for 
monetary policy to stay focused on its price 
stability mandate rather than on ad dressing 
financial stability risks due to fiscal concerns.

On the markets front, e ven as external triggers 
impacted the rupiah, the one -sided sharp 
depreciation run in the currency in the first half 
of the year has stalled . Debt markets have  
attracted offshore interest, alongside strong 
demand for shorter-tenor SRBI securities, all of 
which have contributed to the overall capital 
flows picture.

Ahead of MSCI’s final take on the domestic 
equity market’s classification in November, 
however, equity markets  find little favour . 
Nonetheless, material improvement s in the 
stock listing s and regulatory requirements 
undertaken by the stock market and financial 
regulators are expected to contribute to the 
medium-term stability of the capital markets.

Key domestic risks that plagued the economy 
in the first half of the year have been alleviated 
to some extent, while a few other monitorables 
remain.

• Key domestic policy concerns 
have eased. 
• Policy alignment has improved. 
• Greater fiscal discipline should 
allow BI to remain focused on 
price stability.

widen as the trade surplus 
narrows.

INDONESIA: Policy mix aligns

12 OCTOBER 2026 
Radhika Rao, Senior Economist 
radhikarao@dbs.com

### Page 2

Indonesia: Policy mix aligns October 12, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            2

NEAR-TERM FISCAL WORRIES 
RECEDE

Fiscal fears have moderated in the near 
term. New Finance Minister Suahasil 
Nazara reaffirmed the government’s 
commitment to maintain the annual 
deficit below -3% of GDP threshold. The 
2026 run-rate suggests that the deficit is 
likely to close within the budgeted target.

By September 2026, the fiscal deficit 
stood at 1.2% of GDP , reflecting a 
normalisation in spending and firm 
revenue growth. Total spending rose by 
a still -strong 1 9% yoy , even though the 
pace moderated from 1H, backed by 
disbursements towards the flagship free 
food program, subsidy and 
compensation outlays , as well as 
personnel expenditure.  Realised 
expenditure stood at 69% of the full -
year budget by 9M26.

On the other hand, revenues rose sharply 
by 25 .5% yoy (partly benefiting from 
base effects as readings were negative 
at the same time last year), helped by 
higher tax collections, export duties, and 
non-tax receipts. Revenues have already 
accounted for 74% of the budgeted 
scale by 9M26, suggesting full -year 
target is likely to be met.

Global crude prices are a key risk for the 
fiscal outlook, as prevailing Brent prices 
are above $100bl at the time of writing 
(vs budgeted ICP at $70bl), which will 
feed through higher subsidy and 
compensation outgoes, as authorities 
remain hesitant to raise fuel pump prices.

Significant efficiency measures have, 
however, been put in place  to contain 
spending, including reductions in 
outlays towards flagship schemes , 
which will help contain the extent of 
deterioration in the fiscal math. For 
instance, the flagship MBG program, 
which started with an allocation of IDR 
35trn, was  moderated to IDR 268trn 
(realised spending by Sep26 was IDR 
153trn). Successive stimulus packages

### Page 3

Indonesia: Policy mix aligns October 12, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            3

have been announced but the scale has 
been contained.

2027 math signals fiscal prudence, soft 
spots warrant attention

The official goalposts for 2027 point to 
a continued preference for fiscal 
prudence rather than fiscal activism.

Spending is on course to surpass the 
symbolic IDR 4000tn level, ris ing nearly 
4% yoy, with outlays divided between 
the centre and regional governments. 
The mix points to steady allocations 
towards subsidies, social transfers , and 
capital expenditure,  with ministry -level 
spending (K/L) set to contract, while non-
K/L spending is budgeted to increase. 
More notably, spending is expected to 
rise on key flagship programs – for 
instance, social protection (up 10%), the 
largest component education (up 14%) , 
which includes the free meals program 
at IDR 240trn.

On the revenue end, state revenues are 
expected to rise by 9% yoy compared to 
the budgeted 2026 APBN/ 7% vs the 
revised 2026 outlook. Compared to 
baseline 2026, tax revenues are 
expected to rise 10.5%, besides higher 
non-tax collections. This math lands the 
2027 budgeted deficit at -2.4% of GDP 
vs revised 2026 at -2.85% of GDP.

Few uncertainties that loom.  Firstly, 
next year’s math rests on an upbeat 
growth projection of 6 .0% (DBSf 5.2%) , 
besides modest depreciation in the 
rupiah and about  a 7% increase in the 
Indonesia crude price assumption (see 
table). Some of these expectations 
might be challenged as the year unfolds. 
Next, state revenues are expected to 
rise to a n upbeat  12.5% of GDP , likely 
riding on a strong growth forecast, 
better tax due diligence , and improved 
administration via the Coretax 
mechanism.

Thirdly, crude prices are a key risk to the 
fiscal math. Given the economy’s 
position as a net oil importer, various 
official commentaries put the sensitivity  
at every $1pb oil worsening the fiscal 
balance by IDR 6trn, after accounting for 
oil-related revenues. This implies that for 
every $10pb increase, the deficit could 
be impacted by ~IDR 60trn, not far from 
MOF’s sensitivity of IDR 68trn (~0.25 -
0.30% of GDP) due to higher subsidy and 
compensation costs  (ICT assumed at 
$75bl vs prevailing Brent at $100bl). 
Spending will need to reprioritised to 
accommodate additional allocation 
towards subsidies and compensation.

Next, the direction of travel on the 
contribution of SOE dividends from the 
investment agency to the state coffers 
for 2027 remains unclear, after being 
accounted for this year. Lastly, 
successive stimulus measures in-kind 
and wage subsidies were announced this 
year and could marginally add to the 
strain in 2027 (0.1-0.2% of GDP).

Discussions around the State Finance 
Law also warrant a watch. We maintain 
our deficit forecast at a wider -2.8% to -
2.9% range to account for any revenue 
shortfall or a gradual pace of growth. 
Nonetheless, deficits/debt are likely to 
stay below key thresholds  and not 
emerge as a flashpoint for the markets.

MONETARY POLICY ON AN EVEN 
KEEL

Bank Indonesia has left the benchmark 
rate unchanged  since July 2026, after 
100bp hikes in 1H26. While tighter global 
financial conditions and geopolitical 
risks were key risks on the horizon, the BI 
held the domestic GDP forecast at 4.9 -
5.7% (DBSf: 5.3%) alongside 
manageable inflation, notwithstanding 
El Nino-related risks. Inflation rose to 3.3% 
yoy in Sep26, towards the high end of the 
target range. Authorities have, however, 
assured that the impact of higher food 
prices due to poor weather will be 
addressed through administrative 
measures by regional bodies.

Contained inflation expectations ha ve 
reduced the urgency for BI to tighten 
policy further, allowing policymakers to 
prioritise growth and financial stability 
considerations. The emphasis on 
strengthening the balance of payments 
position reflects continued caution over 
external financing conditions and capital 
flows. However, with the rupiah 
expected to remain stable on the back of 
policy measures and fundamenta ls, the 
BI did not view current external 
pressures as severe enough to warrant 
additional tightening.

BI introduced a host of measures at the 
June to September policy meetings , 
including FX monetary hedging 
instruments and a mechanism to provide 
foreign investors with greater flexibility 
in managing IDR-denominated 
investments in Indonesia.  
These included –  
a) swap hedging per tenor bucket was 
increased to 3M - 15%; 6M - 20%; 12M - 
25%, from 12.5% earlier.  
b) DNDF incentive was increased to 6M - 
25% and 12M – 30%. An increase in FX/ 
NDF swap premium incentives for longer 
tenors will lower hedging costs and

### Page 5

Indonesia: Policy mix aligns October 12, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            5

encourage longer tenor positions, in turn 
increasing longevity of these flows;  
c) Launch of the VASTRA scheme (FX 
Market Transactions for Hedging 
through Partner Banks) got underway, 
with the naming of 14 domestic partner 
banks. This facility is designed to help 
investors transact with lower underlying 
documentation requirements, tap into 
longer banking hours , and access 
onshore hedging facilities.  
d) deepen FX and money markets by 
expanding Local Currency Transactions 
(LCT) between IDR and CNY , besides a 
few other currencies.

Policymakers are likely to tap a 
combination of administrative and 
macroprudential measures rather than 
solely interest rates to defend the 
currency as well as keep inflationary 
expectations in check.

We expect the pause on rates to extend 
into 4Q26.  This call might be at risk if 
depreciation pressures on the rupiah 
mount again on unfavourable global 
developments and if the US Fed 
undertakes a more aggressive hik ing 
cycle than our baseline (of 75bp hikes).

WIDER CAD WILL BE PARTLY 
OFFSET BY POSITIVE FLOWS

Indonesia’s 1H26 current account 
deficit (CAD) widened significantly  to -
$16bn vs -$3bn in the comparable period 
a year ago. Much of this deterioration 
was led by a narrower goods trade 
balance, while  the services deficit 
widened on the seasonal travel account 
due to the Hajj period. Higher coupon 
payments weighed on the primary 
account.  This pressure is likely to persist 
in 2H as the oil & gas trade deficit 
remains wide and export performance 
has been fi rm but tracking lower than 
regional peers, likely also impacted by 
the El Nino phenomenon in key agri 
commodities. We expect full year CAD to 
be closer to 1.8-2% of GDP from -0.1% in 
2025.

Much rests on the capital flows account. 
While 1H balance of payments (BOP) also 
widened, there was palpable 
improvement in 2Q26 vs 1Q.  2Q BOP 
narrowed significantly to $0.9bn from a 
wide $9.1bn in 1Q, helped by positive 
capital account flows – primarily 
stronger portfolio investment on SRBI 
and debt inflows, and net FDI position. 
This dependency is likely to extend to 2H, 
with capital fl ows till Sep26 holding up 
on account of hedging discounts and 
moves to improve inflows. Global 
conditions, including the narrow ID -US 
yields, however, warrant attention. 
Nonetheless, full-year BOP could remain

### Page 6

Indonesia: Policy mix aligns October 12, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            6

wide, on a scale comparable to the 2025 
gap.

IN SUMMARY

IDR markets have been relatively 
steady in October despite higher oil 
prices and a bid dollar , though 
sentiment remains sensitive to external 
developments and swings in global risk 
appetite. USDIDR traded within 17600 -
18000, while 10Y yield hovered around 
7.15-7.25%. A jump in the Aug goods 
trade surplus aided this respite, 
alongside foreign purchases of domestic 
debt, which have remained positive this 
month and on a  YTD basis. Insurers, the 
central bank, and retail investors have 
remained key anchors for the bond 
market. SRBI rates are off highs to sub -
7% levels but are attracting steady 
demand, likely encouraged by hedging 
discounts. An improved outlook for flows 
has helped contain spillovers from a 
wider current account deficit. This does 
not take away from the fact that the 
rupiah is amongst the worst performers 
on a year-to-date basis.

Separately, markets will also monitor 
developments in the political economy, 
institutional architecture, and formation, 
as well as the role of state -led agencies 
(e.g., DSI) and progress on the 
centralisation of decision -making. We 
expect the BI to keep on hold, however, 
pressure to tighten policy might return if 
the US Fed hikes rates well into 2027 
and the US dollar benefits from a risk-off 
environment, weighing on the currency.
